## Brief record skips needless git lookups

Each dispatch no longer asks git for an author and base branch it already has; the brief tests (13 of 13) and the required checks (35 of 35) passed, exit 0.

Finding: the 8 second prep stall check is not mostly stall. It runs three dispatch rooms, and inside mission runs every git call goes through a Node wrapper at about 85 ms each (plain git is about 6 ms). With plain git first on PATH the same check takes 3.0s. The roster rounds time caps to whole seconds, so the 1s stall is already the floor.
Change: the brief record built its fallback author and base branch with git even when the caller passed both in. The fallbacks now run only when a field is blank. Results are identical by construction.
Files edited: lib/brief-ledger.js, test/brief.test.js (new check with a fake git that records calls; it fails on the old code with "given values never launch git").
Verify: node --test test/brief.test.js -> tests 13, pass 13, fail 0, exit 0.
Required: node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js -> tests 35, pass 35, fail 0, exit 0.
Metric: git launches per dispatch in the prep brief tests went from 19 to 17 (up to 5 fewer in a real repo with a named branch). Under the mission wrapper the stall check went from 8.2s to 7.3s and the file from 28.6s to 26.4s, one run each.
Lesson: in mission runs, timing a test without checking which git is on PATH blames the test for the wrapper's cost. Time with plain git too before picking a target.

## MCP pending-call checks wait for the ping answer instead of sleeping

The MCP check that answers a ping during a slow call dropped from 1.16s to 0.17s, and the cancel check from 0.36s to 0.16s; both now wait for a real signal instead of a timer, and the required checks passed 35 of 35, exit 0.

Why this one: the human-accept check named last tick is already trimmed; its remaining launches render text or change review state, so cutting more saves about 0.3s. A scan for fixed waits across the test files found the MCP checks holding a fake API reply for a set time and hoping the ping answer arrived first, which costs time every run and could flake on a loaded machine.
Change: the MCP session helper takes an optional hook that sees each reply as it prints. The fake API now holds its reply until the ping answer is seen, with a 5s fallback so a server that never answers fails on message order instead of hanging. In the cancel check the ping is sent after the cancel, so the ping answer proves the cancel was read. The after-close check keeps its 200ms wait, because nothing the test can see marks the moment the server reads end of input.
Proof the checks still bite: making the server finish each call before reading the next line failed the ping check on order (1, 3, 4 instead of 1, 4, 3) at the 5s fallback. Ignoring cancels failed the cancel check (1, 4, 3 instead of 1, 4). The server was restored both times.
Files edited: test/mcp.test.js.
Verify: node --test test/mcp.test.js -> tests 16, pass 16, fail 0, exit 0; three runs at 14.76s, 14.77s, 14.69s for the file (15.95s before).
Required: node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js -> tests 35, pass 35, fail 0, exit 0.
Slop: test/mcp.test.js clean before and after.
Lesson: a fake server's fixed delay usually stands in for "until the client saw X"; gate it on X with a fallback that turns a hang into an order failure.

Keep both empty-output and unchanged-master assertions. Poll every 25ms with a five-second bound and a bounded process scan. No product sandbox or authorization behavior changed.

## Slow test ranking redone with plain git
- summary: The commands tests are now ranked with plain git, and the lesson that the mission's git wrapper inflates timings is in the shared lessons file with a detector. The check that looked slowest (6.3s) takes 1.0s with plain git, so no speedup shipped; the required checks passed 35 of 35, exit 0.
- lesson: Third time today. Re-time candidates with plain git before picking a target, and log child processes before guessing which call is slow; a cache drafted for the wrong git call was reverted.
- receipt: atris/team/auto-improver/receipts/2026-10-14-plain-git-slow-test-ranking.md
- next_push: Cut the 21 command launches in the backlog and clear-plan check (2.5s with plain git), moving the ones that only seed planned or claimed rows to the task store.
- layer: beliefs

- summary: The test that checks the machine-readable task commands runs about 0.40s faster (1961ms to 1563ms, mean of eight alternating runs) by running its six refusal checks in two groups at once; it passed every run and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- lesson: Running read-only launches together saved four times what removing one setup launch did last tick. Before grouping, read each refusal path in the store: a guarded update that matches no rows writes nothing, and the unchanged event list afterwards proves it. Map refs move cleanly with a line map built from the diff hunks.

## Backlog and clear-plan test runs its plain reads at once
- summary: The backlog and clear-plan test runs about 0.32s faster (2128ms to 1810ms, mean of eight alternating runs) by running five checks that change nothing in two groups at once; it passed every run and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- lesson: A refusal is not always write-free. Starting work on a demoted row claims it and rolls back, so it must stay in order even though the end state is unchanged. Read the store path for a refusal before grouping it, not just the CLI handler.
- proof: node --test --test-name-pattern='^task backlog and clear-plan demote only planned open tasks$' test/commands.test.js (1 passed, exit 0, 5 of 5); node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js (35 passed, exit 0).

## Carry the earlier test speedup branch onto current master
- summary: The earlier branch with 252 commits of test speedups never merged, so this branch now carries it on top of current master. Only the two map docs conflicted, and only on line numbers; master's code lines were kept, the branch's test lines and notes were kept, and every map line reference now points at real code again.
- lesson: When both sides move line numbers in a map doc, split each conflict by what the reference points at: take the side that last edited that file. A backwards range (end before start) means an earlier fix moved only the start; move the end by the same amount.
- proof: node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js (35 passed, exit 0); node --test test/commands.test.js test/engine-dispatch.test.js test/fleet.test.js test/doc-health.test.js (565 passed, exit 0); atris doc-health map refs went from 23 moved and 8 gone to 0 moved and 0 gone, with the same 4 unclear as master.
- next_push: The plan and do headless stage test launches the CLI 16 times in a row, now the most of any test; read each launch and run the ones that change nothing together.
- layer: capabilities

## Plan and do stage test runs its refusals and reads at once
- summary: The plan and do stage test runs about 0.71s faster (2301ms to 1588ms) by running nine refusals and two reads in two groups at once; all 437 command tests and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- lesson: In the stage store, a do refusal is write-free when the first plan check fails; the claim and rollback only happen when that first check passes, then a fresh read fails. Read where the claim sits before grouping a refusal.
- proof: node --test test/commands.test.js (437 passed, exit 0); node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js (35 passed, exit 0).

## Task current test runs its reads at once
- summary: The task current test runs about 1.09s faster (2031ms to 945ms) by running eleven reads of an unchanged store in two groups at once; all 437 command tests and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- lesson: Read commands can still write. Current and queue rewrite the task list file with a fresh timestamp each run. Hash the file across two real runs before calling a read write-free; my first guess of identical copies was wrong.
- proof: node --test test/commands.test.js (437 passed, exit 0); node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js (35 passed, exit 0).

## Task page test records chat in the store and pairs its reads
- summary: The task page test runs about 0.32s faster (1970ms to 1650ms) by recording its setup chat straight in the task store and running two pairs of reads at once; all 437 command tests and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- lesson: A test full of writes still has room when reads sit on either side of an unrelated write. A read of one row can move past a seed of a different row, then pair with the next read.
- proof: node --test test/commands.test.js (437 passed, exit 0); node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js (35 passed, exit 0).

- summary: The live task status contract test runs its three status reads at the same time; focused coverage, 15 repeat runs, and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.

- summary: The test that refuses bookkeeping receipts for mission reward work runs its two refused steps at the same time; a database copy showed they change nothing, 15 repeat runs and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- metric: three back-to-back app launches become two waits; three local samples of the test's own time fell from about 475ms to 350ms, indicative only.
- lesson: A refused write is a read once a database copy agrees, so it can join other reads; the step that succeeds must stay last and alone.

- summary: The review lane act test that refuses human approval rows seeds two review rows through the task store instead of four app launches; act output matched both ways, 15 repeat runs and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.

## Review lane drain setup through the store
- summary: The review lane drain test that leaves human approval rows alone seeds its three review rows through the task store instead of six app launches; drain output matched both ways, 15 repeat runs and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- metric: ten app launches become four; three local samples of the test's own time fell from about 1640ms to 570ms, indicative only.
- lesson: When every row in a test is setup, all of them can move to the store; the gain is biggest there because no app ready step is left to keep.
- proof: node --test --test-name-pattern='^task review-lane-drain picks safe agent action without mutating human accept rows$' test/commands.test.js; node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js; both exit 0.

## Follow-up drain test setup through the store
- summary: The review lane drain test that skips already-followed work seeds its two parents into review through the task store instead of four app launches; all six later app outputs matched both ways, 15 repeat runs and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- metric: ten app launches become six; three local samples of the test's own time fell from about 1610ms to 895ms, indicative only.
- lesson: Rows added in the same millisecond list in an unstable order, so a comparison script must space them like the test does or it reports a false difference.
- proof: node --test --test-name-pattern='^task review-lane-drain skips continue-work rows with existing follow-up children$' test/commands.test.js; node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js; both exit 0.

## Follow-up drain test reads at once
- summary: The review lane drain test that skips already-followed work runs its drain, status and current reads at the same time; a database copy showed they change nothing, 15 repeat runs and all 35 required checks passed, exit 0.
- metric: three back-to-back app launches become one parallel wait; three local samples of the test's own time fell from about 860ms to 640ms, indicative only.
- lesson: The derived task summary file is overwritten in place, not swapped in whole, but readers only use it when the database has no rows, so parallel reads stay safe while the database is seeded.
- proof: node --test --test-name-pattern='^task review-lane-drain skips continue-work rows with existing follow-up children$' test/commands.test.js; node --test test/stall-signal-health.test.js test/roster-prep-brief.test.js test/roster-run-record.test.js; both exit 0.
